# Trapped in the Internet: the tech checklist

> **Build history (2026-10-24).** The audit checklist from 2026-10-14, kept
> through wave 20 and not ticked for the device checks. It still describes
> retired parts (the ESP32 patch bay, the chess phone, QLab, one "house
> Mac"). **For the night use [`SHOWNIGHT.md`](./SHOWNIGHT.md) and
> [`TECH.md`](./TECH.md).** After the party it is the backlog for
> [`SCHEDULE.md`](./SCHEDULE.md).

Everything, software and hardware, that has to be true on the night for the story in
`Trapped In The Internet story/` (STORY BEATS, Story Overview, Character Sheet, Clippy
Instructions) to run in this house. It came from a full audit of the codebase against the
story on 2026-10-14 (commit `6884a96`) and from the rewrite spec, kept next to the story as
[`DESIGN.md`](./DESIGN.md) ("spec §N" below; its §8 print appendix and §9 runbook live in
[`README.md`](./README.md)). The rewrite itself (section 0), `stagehand/trapped.house.yaml`
and its test are committed (`fba382b`).

> **🎃 The party is Sat Oct 31, 2026. The active plan is [`OCT31.md`](./OCT31.md)**: 29.6 lane-days in 4 lanes (27.6 committed + 2.0 conditional for iPhone push, K-31b), gates Oct 18 / 20 / 21 / 22 / 23, push call Oct 24 12:00, run-through Oct 25, freeze Oct 27, tech 10-28, dress 10-29, code freeze 10-30. Every open item below carries an **Oct 31 tag** after its owner: **🎃** in for Oct 31 (`K-xx`/`N-xx` = a crunch build, `B-x`/`P-x` = a hardware or production block, `OP-xx` = a night-of procedure, `D-01` = cast paper), **🩹 `W-xx`** runs on that workaround from OCT31.md §9, **⭐** optional, **💤** after the party. "(min)" = only the minimal slice named in OCT31.md §5.

**Schedule: see [`SCHEDULE.md`](./SCHEDULE.md)** for the full post-party roadmap: the order, lanes, gates and cut line that every open P0/P1 item here is built in (each id sits in exactly one work package there).

Lies told to Trabolta in a DM count (#33). Dropped items stay ~~struck through~~ with the reason.

Recount after every edit (`- [ ] **ID** <status> <priority>` and `- [x] **ID**` lines; tags = the `· 🎃`/`· 🩹`/`· ⭐`/`· 💤` before the ` — `).

## How to keep this living

- **Tick boxes in the PR that does the work.** Flip `- [ ]` to `- [x]`, change the status
  emoji, and append the PR or commit (`— #123` / `— 1a2b3c4`). An item is ✅ only when it is
  merged **and** verified (a test that runs it, or a live run on real devices, noted inline).
- **Never renumber.** Ids are stable (`SW-SCR-01`, `HW-DOOR-02`, …). New items take the next
  free number in their section. A dropped item stays, struck through, with a reason.
- **Owners:** replace `@__` with initials when someone picks an item up.
- **Re-date the "Last reviewed" line** whenever you walk the whole list (at least weekly
  until the party, and after every rehearsal).
- **Paths** are relative to `packages/loom/` unless they start with `./` (this folder).
  Line numbers are as of `6884a96` and drift; the file + symbol is the stable part.

### Legend

| Mark | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ | Exists **and** verified (a passing test or a recorded live run, cited) |
| 🟡 | Exists but unverified live for this show, or only partly there / workaround only |
| 🔴 | Missing: needs new code, content, a purchase or a build |
| **P0** | The party cannot run without it |
| **P1** | Big loss of the designed experience if missing |
| **P2** | Polish / stretch |
| **S / M / L** | Size: S ≤ half a day · M ≈ 1–3 days · L ≈ a week or more |
| ~~struck~~ | Dropped or merged into another item (reason inline). No checkbox, not counted |
| 🎃 `K-xx` | **Oct 31:** in. Built in the crunch (`K-`/`N-`), by you + friends (`B-`/`P-`), or a night-of procedure (`OP-`), per [`OCT31.md`](./OCT31.md) |
| 🎃 `wave 4 X·n` | **Oct 31 wave 4:** built 2026-10-16, committed as 9d35a4b (not deployed); `X·n` is the lane · ask row of OCT31.md §5 *Wave 4 status* that carries its device check |
| 🎃 `wave 5 Xn` | **Oct 31 wave 5:** built 2026-10-16, committed as 85d2bad (not deployed); `Xn` is the lane · item row of OCT31.md §5 *Wave 5 status* that carries its device check |
| 🎃 `wave 19` | **Oct 31 wave 19:** the voice link + the in-headset program picker, built 2026-10-21 on `oct31-w19/integration` (not deployed); the device checks are in OCT31.md §5 *Wave 19 status* |
| 🎃 `wave 18b` | **Oct 31 wave 18b:** the rehearsal fixes, founded factions, results on the screens, the house router and the tent laptop, built 2026-10-22 (Stage A + the tent lane shipped as `w18b-1021`; Stage B on `oct31-w18/integ`, not shipped); the device checks are in OCT31.md §5 *Wave 18b status*, the decisions in `SIGNOFF-w18b.md` |
| 🩹 `W-xx` | **Oct 31:** runs on that workaround (OCT31.md §9); the full build is post-party (SCHEDULE.md) |
| ⭐ | **Oct 31:** optional, only on slack |
| 💤 | **Oct 31:** after the party; not needed on the night |

Item line format: `id status priority · size · owner · Oct 31 tag — what, and the story moment it serves → where to change it`.

## Critical path: do these first, in this order

*For Oct 31 the order is OCT31.md's day-by-day table (§4); this list is the full-build order for the next run.*

1. **SW-QR-01** Fix the printed-QR base URL (`joinBase` ignores `LOOM_BASE_URL`), before anything is printed. *(K-01 on master, not yet deployed.)*
2. **SW-OPS-02** Real HTTPS on the **VPS** (SW-OPS-01 decided: VPS, #16). The camera, wake lock, Web Push and the Quest's network access all depend on it, and so does every printed URL. Right after it: **SW-OPS-18** (stagehand + the agents worker connect outbound from the house to the VPS). *(SW-AUD-08, Kokoro on the VPS, was dropped 2026-10-15: native device voices.)*
3. ~~**SW-STORY-01..05, 07..10, 12** Finish the `.loom` rewrite and its tests~~ — done and committed (`fba382b`). **Left:** **SW-STORY-06** push it to the live preview, run the two e2e-live specs (SW-STORY-04 / 08), and wire `loomcheck` into CI.
4. **SW-MAP-01** Make the house map a project file *before* anyone spends hours surveying. Today a save lives in one event's state dir and is lost on End → relaunch.
5. **SW-MAP-02 / SW-MAP-07 / SW-MAP-13** Measure the house (since Oct 31 wave 3 the placeholder already has the real rooms on 3 floors, so the survey corrects footprints, positions and elevations; stairs side by side or end to end, never stacked): real rooms, the **Server Room**, the tent **outside** in the yard and the **shed** as its rain fallback (#23/#31), the Truth Pong table and every terminal mount. Then run `pnpm house:sync` and `pnpm test:vr`.
6. **SW-PLAY-01** Featured-program claims, so only Rob can be Microsoft Excel. (~~Trojan Horse shared identity~~: dropped, #24.)
7. **SW-SCR-01** Terminal attract mode before Trabolta exists: no "TRABOLTA ONLINE" and no piloting into `dm:Trabolta`. *(K-16 on master, not yet deployed; **SW-SCR-02**, the `view=mirror` runbook, is only the fallback now.)*
8. **SW-SH-01 / SW-SH-02** `stagehand/trapped.house.yaml` and its cross-check test are committed (`fba382b`, 12 tests green). Add the reed-switch and door-indicator rules (no maglock, #17), a `blackout` group that spares the stairs, bathroom and tent, then verify on the real rig. *(Wave 7: the door contact is the wire loops that touch when the door opens, and the real lock is a standalone keypad nothing drives, #17 amended.)*
9. **HW-DOOR-01..05 + HW-PATCH-01..07** (wave 20: the door sensor is HW-DOOR-10 / 11, the M4's LED + LDR) Build the server-room door sensor (wave 7: the wire-loop contact that closes when the door opens, HW-DOOR-06; the reed optional), fit the standalone keypad lock (HW-DOOR-09), and the patch-bay scanner (digital all-pairs, #18), and bench-test them against stagehand. Use the MQTT topics in this file, which match the YAML (`sensors/patchbay/state`, `sensors/serverroom/door`, `show/serverroom/lock`).
10. **SW-SCR-03 / 05 / 06 / 14** A minimal Screens layer: a cue bridge, the screensaver, the blackout and "GO TO THE LIVING ROOM" takeover, plus countdowns (**SW-SCR-09**).
11. **SW-PLAY-19 / 20 / 22 + SW-OPS-17, then SW-PLAY-03 / SW-PLAY-04** Web Push for every phone (#25; the iOS install-first door flow changes the signage, so it must land before printing), performer alerts and the phone takeover. *(Android + booths: K-31a on master; iPhone is K-31b, conditional.)*
12. **SW-QR-02 / 03 / 11 / 14** Pinned codes, a redeem throttle, and the Run → Print page *(K-10 / K-11 / K-12 on master)*. Then print a test sheet and scan it on cellular data, together with the "Add to Home Screen" door sign (SW-OPS-17).
13. **SW-AUD-01 / 02 + HW-AUD-07 / 08** The room-audio router on the TV computer and its 16-out interface (#13/#14): every `sound` plays in the right rooms from local files.
14. **SW-RE-01 / 02 / 03** The first real Quest APK, the first real tablet run, and a live re-run of the review-round fixes.
15. **SW-OPS-06 / 07** A full dress rehearsal with friends, following the checklist at the end of Ops.
16. **SW-OPS-11 + HW-SAFE-01..05** Code freeze and a pinned production deploy; the safety walk (darkness, strobes, mains, outdoor power) before guests arrive.

## 1. Screens and terminals (the "monitors")

The house's monitors are **old Android tablets** (whatever turns up: mixed Android versions and WebViews; Fully Kiosk where it runs, plain Chrome elsewhere, #15) running the Terminal app (`terminal/`), plus the TV above the fireplace, which is **Trabolta's main display** (his face and voice for Desktop lines, the slides, the reinstall mirror, #13). The TV is driven by a **TV computer** (a Mac, HW-AUD-07) that also runs the 16-channel room-audio router (section 11). Terminals still play their own audio, speech and video where the house speakers don't reach (SW-AUD-09). Before Trabolta is introduced they are screensavers, a media player and an ad rotation. After that they show story beats and videos, show guests reinstalling, and become his line.
Today the terminal does exactly two things: it pilots a scanned guest's `dm:Trabolta` thread with a face and a voice, and it spectates the reinstall game (`view=auto|world|face|mirror`, `terminal/src/config.ts:46`). Nothing on the screens reacts to `cue screens_*` (the new `stagehand/trapped.house.yaml` turns `screens_flicker` into a house light blink, but no screen consumes any `screens_*` cue).

## 4. QR codes

The 41 coded clues (spec §8.3) fill the Codex. Room posters check people in. Pre-baked, printable QRs belong to guests, performers, clues, locations and devices.

- [ ] **SW-QR-01** 🟡 P0 · S · @__ · 🎃 K-01 — **Printed base-URL bug.** — **Oct 31 wave 1:** on master (not yet deployed) (K-01 `pickJoinBase`, `LOOM_BASE_URL` wins, 334c11f), device check: rebuild the VPS, `scripts/accept/k01.sh` green, an iPhone on cellular scans the Run join QR and lands on https://app.mapsandducks.com.
  - The problem: `joinBase()` (`core/server/server.ts:147`) uses the first LAN interface (`http://<ipv4>:7000`) and ignores `LOOM_BASE_URL` (`core/server/config.ts:19`). It feeds `joinUrl` (`core/server/events-api.ts:54`) and every codex URL (`/api/mod/codes`, `event-runtime.ts:2645`). In Docker that prints `http://172.x.y.z:7000/?code=…`: unreachable, plain http, and it bypasses Caddy. `docs/loom-docker-deploy.md:474-476` claims otherwise.
  - The fix: `LOOM_PUBLIC_URL ?? LOOM_BASE_URL ?? LAN guess`, plus a docs fix. Printed links must never contain an IP or a port.

## 5. Floor plan editor and the house map

One `house.json` (schema v1, `house/src/types.ts`, `validateHouse` in `house/src/validate.ts`) feeds the VR game, the web game and the terminals. The VR game fetches it live (`reinstall/vr/net/server_link.gd`, refetched every 2 s while stale), so a save reaches the headsets with no Godot rebuild. The current map is a **placeholder** (`./house.json` says so).

It can drag, aim, add and remove terminals and duck spots, drag the spawn, move/rotate props, move/resize **rectangular** rooms, set a room's label/LOCATION/tint, edit the raw JSON with live validation, and save with `base` → 409 on a conflict. The 409 path is unit-tested only, never re-run live. — **Oct 31 wave 2:** on master (not yet deployed) (edc7314, 42b36b8, review fixes c35b7b0): `MapApp.tsx` is split into `Canvas` / `Toolbar` / `Inspector` / `Panels` / `tools.ts` over pure `doors` / `yards` / `structure` / `history` / `check` / `ids` modules (every edit is a pure function, so undo / redo are stacks of maps); map tests 33 → 77 (`map.test.ts` + `map-structure.test.ts`). The 409 path is still unit-tested only. The structural tools are SW-MAP-03.

Order: a director's save > the project's active variant > `LOOM_HOUSE` (now only a fallback). **Save to project** = `POST /api/mod/spatial/house/project` (writes the playing variant's file); every map write is validated (PUT `/files`, `mod/load {maps}`, the sync). `sync-example` / `scripts/sync-show.sh` push the maps but **the project's copy wins** (only missing example maps are added; `--replace-maps` overwrites, with backups; `--keep-maps` skips). *Download JSON* is in `reinstall/web/src/map/ProjectBar.tsx`. **Deploy the server before the first sync-show that carries maps** (an older server reads `house.json` as story).   - The fix: make `house.json` a `project_file`, snapshot it at launch like `scenario_source`, resolve the event map as director save > project file > `LOOM_HOUSE`/show file (`core/server/event-runtime.ts` `spatialHouse()`), add "Save to project" + "Download JSON" on the map page, and have `core/scripts/sync-example.ts` push `house.json`.

  - per floor: elevation and ceiling;

  - solid furniture;
  - **every terminal mount** (pos, yaw, pitch, fov), so the "window" lines up;
  - 6–10 duck spots: none in the spawn room, none buried in a prop, ≤ 1.2 m up, **none in the Server Room**.

## 7. Trabolta and the agents

### How he changes over the night (inaccessible → guarded → influenced) and what makes it happen

| Phase (`Night.phase`) | Guests experience | Tech | Items |
|---|---|---|---|
| arrivals … bingo_results | **Inaccessible.** He doesn't exist on any phone. Terminals are screensavers. | `hidden: true` (`core/src/runtime/sim/sim.ts` `peopleFor` / `isRevealedTo`); worker gate `when: self.awake == true` → hold line (`stagehand/src/stagehand/agents/module.py`); terminals must not pilot | SW-SCR-01/02, SW-AI-11 |
| introduced, hunt_1 | **Guarded / formal.** Introduced by Clippy; DMs are corporate; personal questions are routed to Clippy | `introduce_trabolta` → `awake = true`, `reveal self`; stage `formal` | SW-AI-03 |
| truth_1 | **Hungry.** Warmer to those who deliver facts; pays T1 lore | shares (`act.share`), `mint_truthcoin` | SW-AI-05 |
| malware, hunt_2, summons | **Rattled.** Leaks and reassures; paranoid | + `cut_the_lights`, `summon_the_antivirus`, `trabolta_announces` | SW-AI-14, SW-SH-01 |
| truth_pong, trust_chart | **Judge.** Every DM is an audition; tells you your rank | + `assess` (±10 → TRUST FORMULA), `pardon_a_program` | SW-SCR-11, SW-AI-04 |
| beans, overshare | **Unravelling.** Overshares (Sandy), cold to the LiveJournal reader; screens flicker on their own | T2 lore; involuntary `screens_flicker` | SW-SCR-14 |
| y2k, majority | **Desperate.** Bargains with everyone; loses the screens | + `turn_back_the_clock`; `flicker` refused | SW-SCR-10 |
| final_reveal … finale_* | **Last act.** Scripted endings; only `assess` / `the_line_works` live | phase-guarded power hooks | SW-AI-07 |

**Influenced:** guests move him through DM facts (orchestrator verdicts → dossier trust), shared codex (`when share:` → `Trabolta.truth += 2`; the LiveJournal hook — he no longer takes a hunt answer, 2026-10-19), confident lies at a Moment of Truth (he can't tell them apart: `Kernel.*` is hidden from him), flattery, and relationship advice (`love` / `regard`, read by the director when choosing help vs thwart). **Confident lies told in his DM count too** (decided #33): a guest files a fact (SW-PLAY-24), the server judges it against the hidden `QUESTION` key (SW-AI-20) and bumps `Kernel.lies_accepted`, and Trabolta never sees the bookkeeping (SW-AI-21). He stays hidden until Clippy introduces him; he doesn't reveal himself early to anyone (decided #32, as specced). **Guests' influence never changes a branch by itself** (spec §10.2 #9).

  - the worker with persona gate, mind, orchestrator, trace and control channel (`stagehand/src/stagehand/agents/`; 166 pytest, re-run 2026-10-14);
  - the Run → Mind page driven live with a real worker on both Ollama models (`editor/e2e-live/mind.spec.ts`).

## 8. Story engine gaps (`core/`)

Every item has a working story-side workaround already in the spec; these remove the workaround.

- [ ] **SW-ENG-01** 🔴 P1 · S · @__ · 🩹 W-04 — **`unlock X for group(G)` / `for everyone`.** Today the group form **leaks as public narration of the literal line**, and `for everyone` silently does nothing. Also make `start quest X for everyone` reach later arrivals. Workaround: per-member ROLE watchers + quest catch-up watchers (spec §0 rule 17).
- [ ] **SW-ENG-02** 🔴 P1 · S · @__ · 🩹 W-04 — **`move who to {expr}`** interpolation (today it moves to a literal `{at}` room), and binding **inexact** strings to entities (today a quoted or lowercase place binds as `event#N.at`). Workaround: the check-in if-chain over real LOCATIONs (spec D1, verified).
- [ ] **SW-ENG-03** 🟡 P1 · M · @__ · 🩹 W-05 — **INTERACTION visibility / availability** (`when:` on the declaration, filtered in `guestView` / `PrimeView`), so judging, pong, bingo and pickup buttons appear only in their phase, and the Bathroom toggle flips its label. Workaround: every hook guards its phase; every button is always visible. — **Oct 31 wave 3:** on master (not yet deployed) (8b3d3e0): INTERACTION `while:` (a read-only condition, `Sim.holds`) and `by:` (characters or a GROUP; owners of a `when` for the event count too). Views mark each button `live`; a guest view drops non-live guest buttons (Bathroom Mode or Back, never both; Insert a disk only with a virus), and a performer's guest card leads with that character's live buttons and folds the rest under "More…". Nothing is enforced (the hooks still guard their phase), and a performer button's `while:` is evaluated per booth, not per guest. Device check: Norton's card on a phone.
- [ ] **SW-ENG-04** 🔴 P1 · S · @__ · 🩹 W-04 — **`Persons` excluding Run-mode personas** from majorities, plus `max` / `len` / `top()` / `bottom()` builtins (the ranking the ceremony's awards reuse, SW-ENG-16) (`core/src/runtime/sim/sim.ts:2408-2428`; `len()` is null today). Workaround: accept personas in rehearsal counts; argmax by `count([p … where p.trust > self.trust]) == 0` watchers.
- [ ] **SW-ENG-05** 🔴 P1 · S · @__ · 🩹 W-04 — **`/api/guest/join` refuses hidden / `joinable: false` groups** (`sim.ts:293-313` has no check). Today a crafted request could join Unlimited Power before `y2k_clock` reveals it.
- [ ] **SW-ENG-06** 🔴 P1 · M · @__ · 🩹 W-06 — **Server-held timers + a show clock** in the language (`start timer The Hunt for 15m`, `when timer The Hunt ends:`, `when timer X has 5m left:`), feeding `GuestView` / `ModView` timers and the screen countdowns. Workaround: `Night.x_end = Time.elapsed + N` deadline watchers with phase guards.
- [ ] **SW-ENG-07** 🔴 P2 · M · @__ · 💤 after — **Typed-chat triggers** `when program says "…" in dm:X` (spec §7 #26). Not needed once SW-PLAY-09 lands.
- ~~**SW-ENG-08** 🔴 P0 · S — A **staff scope**: a `performer(X)` / `staff(X)` audience, alert-capable (pairs with SW-PLAY-12).~~ **Dropped 2026-10-16 (wave 5):** booth cues ride the Enforcer readouts, and K-31b's booths use the instruction-only path, so performer push no longer depends on it.
- [ ] **SW-ENG-09** 🔴 P2 · S · @__ · 💤 after — `{self.name}` prints `null` inside CHARACTER hooks/owned beats (spec §0 rule 18). Give characters a `.name`.
- [ ] **SW-ENG-10** 🔴 P2 · S · @__ · 💤 after — **Lint**: warn when a `Capitalised Words:` line headed by a guest-pickable CHARACTER flips it to a performer part (`core/server/identity.ts` `isSpecial`). One stray `VLC:` line breaks the featured design. `identity.test` is the regression guard until then.

- [ ] **SW-ENG-12** 🔴 P2 · S · @__ · 💤 after — Count performers as "programs" in majorities if the user wants OV's "everyone in the room is a program" (spec §10.2 #11, an engine limit today).

- [ ] **SW-ENG-18** 🔴 P1 · S · @__ · 🩹 W-06 — **`at` (server time) on every journal line** (found by the schedule review): `JournalEntry` is `{m, a, by}` (`core/server/store.ts:54-58`) and `SimEvent` carries no time, so the recap (SW-AI-17) can't compute bathroom minutes, "q4 lie via DM at 21:43" or any time-based stat. Stamp `at` on append; replay ignores it. Must land **before the first live-preview night** so that run's journal can feed the recap.

  - a rule for the **reed switch** (`sensors/serverroom/door`, `when: "open == true"` → `server_door_opened`); today the primary sensor would fire nothing (HW-DOOR-01). The copper-tape fallback (HW-DOOR-06) publishes on the same topic from the same ESP32, so it needs no extra rule (#17);

  - `sound` cues move from QLab OSC to the room-audio router (SW-AUD-01); keep the QLab rules as the fallback until rehearsal passes; — **Wave 18b:** done the other way: QLab is not part of the rig; the 19 OSC sfx rules, the `duck_music` rule, the QLab target and the `alexa:` section are deleted, and the router (`audio:`, `play_sounds` + `announce` on) plays every story sound and announcement (SW-AUD-14);
  - `lights_blackout` (and every whole-house darkening) on a **`blackout` group** that leaves out the stairs, the bathroom and the tent; today it targets `house` = everything (HW-SAFE-01, HW-LIT-06). **Oct 31 (K-05, critic pass):** stronger: take the stairs, bathroom, tent, server room and the server-room → living-room path **out of `house` entirely** (scenes 12/13/15/18 and the alarm recall on `house`), and replace `wled/all off` with per-device offs that leave `wled/tube` (the hall + stair landing strip) on a dim static preset;
  - fix the header comment "same ID in every group": whole-house scenes must be stored on the `house` group itself (HW-LIT-02);
  - commit it; then run each rule on the rig (HW-LIT-02, HW-DOOR-03, HW-PATCH-07).
  - Do **not** edit `show.example.yaml`: `test_config.py` pins it, and its rules are the wrong shape for Z2M.
  - The original contents spec (spec §2.15), all now present in the YAML except where noted above:
    - **room lights**: one rule per allow-listed LOCATION for `<cue: lights, room: {room}, state: off|on>` → `zigbee2mqtt/<room-group>/set {"state": "{state}", "transition": 0}` + the WLED segment, `retain: false`;
    - **scenes**: one rule per `cue lights_*` → Z2M `scene_recall` + WLED `{"ps": N}` (the HW-C scene table);
    - `screens_flicker` → also a light blink;
    - `sfx` (from `sound`) → QLab OSC `/cue/<name>/start` or the mpv module;
    - `announce` → `show/announce`;
    - `door` (target `server_room`) → the indicator topic (no maglock, #17);
    - **sensors** → `server_door_opened`, `printer_button_pressed`, `printer_opened`, `patch_combo_a` / `patch_combo_b` / `patch_rejected` (stagehand judges the combos until SW-SH-03).
    - **Quote `"on"`** in every WLED payload.

Fail: coalesce guest + booth snapshots to ≤ 2/s (extends SW-OPS-21) in the 10-24 buffer. — **Oct 31 wave 1:** on master (not yet deployed) (K-32 `scripts/accept/k32-load.ts` + `GET /api/mod/metrics`, b6b8921/1335045), device check: `node --no-warnings scripts/accept/k32-load.ts --code <rehearsal MOD> --out k32.json --reset-after` → 4 ✔, paste the `record:` line here. — **Wave 5:** the contingency is built and off: `LOOM_SNAPSHOT_MS` (default 0) coalesces guest + booth snapshots. Run the load test with it unset first; only if it fails, set `LOOM_SNAPSHOT_MS=500` in the VPS `.env`, then re-run the load test and the phone check. — **Wave 8:** a stalled SSE client (no bytes leaving its queue for 15 s while over 256 KB, 2 MB for a full mod stream) is dropped and reconnects fresh; a director console that is behind skips Run views and gets the newest. A restart's replay is about 10× faster (the 40-guest, 4,252-line journal: 101 s → about 4–7 s), still about 1 s per 1,500 journal lines end to end. — **Wave 9:** Run's director console takes keyed snapshot patches (`?delta=1`): ≈ 0.12 Mbit/s per console at 45 guests (was 5.9), and `k32-load` now judges each director console at ≤ 1 Mbit/s (the LTE failover's budget); `--full-directors` measures the old way.

## 11. Room audio and the TV computer (`stagehand` `audio` module)

Decided #13/#14: the living-room TV is Trabolta's display, and its computer has a 16-channel output wired to the living room, kitchen and the other rooms. A new stagehand **`audio`** module (Python: `sounddevice` + `soundfile` + `numpy`) opens that one 16-channel output on the TV computer and mixes every cue itself, room by room. The YAML maps each room to output channels (`living [1,2]`, `kitchen [3,4]`, …, reusing the Z2M room names), with per-bus volume, ducking and stop-all. Chrome on the same machine keeps channels 1–2 for the TV's own sound (Trabolta's voice, video soundtracks), so anything that must match the picture never goes through the router. Terminals still make sound where the speakers don't reach (the tent, the bathroom, the shed fallback). Full design: the session's `audio.md`. Rejected: browser Web Audio for all 16 channels, and QLab as the main path (QLab stays the fallback until rehearsal passes). Three things are unverified on real hardware: Kokoro's speed on the chosen VPS plan, whether the interface has 16 analog outs or 8 + ADAT, and QLab's licence tier.

## 12. The chess phone (`chess/`, the Cold Lock disarm path)

> **Wave 11 (2026-10-18):** there is no chess phone. The King's chess is **`@loom/chess`** (`chess/`) played on **the Server Door terminal** outside the server-room door (`view=chess`, HW-CHESS-02), awake all night with no hints; his strength is his shared health in the reinstallation games (SW-KING-06); a checkmate gives *The Door Code* any time before 📄. The items below keep their history.

> **Wave 7 (2026-10-17):** there is no Cold Lock and no disarm any more. A win from `Night.step >= 14` gives the winner the server-room door code (*The Door Code*); the door has a standalone keypad lock (HW-DOOR-09) and never alarms. The original design follows for the record.

Decided #29: a phone taped to the wall outside the server room runs a chess game against a very strong engine. Beating it from `Night.step >= 14` opens the door for 90 s without the alarm. Design: the session's `chess.md`. The engine is **Stockfish 19 "lite single-threaded" WASM** (`stockfish@19.0.1`, ≈ 1.7 MB, no SharedArrayBuffer or COOP/COEP, needs Chrome/WebView 91+) in a Web Worker, falling back to its asm.js build (3.1 MB) on older phones. The rules come from **chess.js** (BSD-2) and the board from **cm-chessboard** (MIT; not react-chessboard 5, which needs React 19, and not chessground, which is GPL-3). **License:** the `chess/` app stays MIT, and the engine lives in its own GPL-3.0-or-later package (`chess/engine/`, like `server/`), talking only UCI over `postMessage`. The VPS serves COPYING and a link to the exact upstream source tag. The default mode is **"Checkmate Trabolta"**: 3 rounds, each a forced mate-in-2-to-4 from the Lichess puzzle database (CC0), with full-strength Stockfish defending; mate within N moves or wait out a 45 s cooldown. An attempt takes 2–4 minutes, so people can take turns. **Oct 31 wave 4 (9d35a4b):** the disarm moved into the simulation — the King at the server-room door on the tent tablet (SW-KING-01..05); the phone stays the fallback until the King is device-checked (U17).

- [ ] **SW-CHESS-01** 🟡 P1 · S · @__ · 🎃 K-27 — **Story** (closes SW-STORY-14): `when chess won / lost / drawn` rules; a win at `Night.step >= 14` sets `Kernel.disarmed_until = Time.elapsed + 90`, cues `lights_server_open` and turns the ring green (`do door server_room, state: unlocked`); the `server_door_opened` rule gets one clause (no alarm while the window is open); a watcher re-locks the door when the window closes (a door left propped open can then trip the alarm: note it in the runbook); Power On init; award counters for the ceremony; a *The Cold Lock* codex entry; interplay with `rearm_server_room` (SW-ADM-10); a test in `trapped-in-the-internet.test` (win at step 14 → the door opens quietly; after 90 s → alarm). Wins before step 14 are practice only. — **Oct 31 wave 1:** on master (not yet deployed) (K-27 min: `when chess_won:` 90 s window from step 14; lost/drawn rules, award counters and the Cold Lock codex left out, 68657d2), device check: `scripts/accept/K-27.md` — ♟ then two reed openings inside 90 s, no alarm. — **Oct 31 wave 2:** the wave 1 leftovers (d58803e) added the `chess lost` / `chess drawn` rules and *The Cold Lock* codex, and no ceremony award uses chess, so nothing is left in code: device check only (K-27). — **Wave 7 (7b446ff, superseded):** the 90 s window is gone with the keypad lock: `when chess_won for program:` (a subject rule) from step 14 unlocks *The Door Code* for the winner, once (a duplicate only notes `# hosts`; no subject → a `# hosts` reminder to press ♟); before it practice. *The Cold Lock* became the secret riddle *The Cold Door*. A lost / drawn game posts a readout every time and gloats at most once per 300 s (`Night.gloat_at`). Device check: `scripts/accept/K-27.ts chess --who` + `K-27.md`. — **Wave 11:** no Y2K gate: `chess_won` gives *The Door Code* any time before 📄 truth.txt (`Night.king_armed` 1 from Power On), the board is the Server Door terminal (SW-CHESS-05), and a loss / draw readout posts all night (the TV gloat only once Trabolta is awake). The riddle is gone; the four digit notes are the other path (HW-PROP-13).

- [ ] **HW-NET-01** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 B-9 — Show router: a **2.4 GHz-only IoT SSID** (WPA2, no band steering, no WPA3-only) for ESP32s, WLED and tablets.
  - Force the 2.4 GHz band to **20 MHz channel width**: a 40 MHz (HT40) channel lands on the Zigbee channel whatever Wi-Fi channel you pick.
  - Set the IoT SSID to **802.11b/g/n with 11ax off on 2.4 GHz** (no TWT/OFDMA; PMF optional, not required). Older ESP32/ESP8266 firmware and Fire OS tablets are known to fail to join Wi-Fi 6 APs, and the GL-MT3000 is one.
  - **Choose the Zigbee channel when the network is created** (every bulb is being reset and re-paired anyway): 15, 20 or 25 sit between Wi-Fi 1/6/11; avoid 26 (power-limited, some bulbs dislike it). Then pick the Wi-Fi channel away from it (Zigbee 15 → Wi-Fi 11; Zigbee 20/25 → Wi-Fi 1). Changing the Zigbee channel after pairing means re-pairing everything.
  - Guests on a separate SSID or cellular.
- [ ] **HW-NET-02** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 B-9 — **DHCP reservations / static IPs** for the TV computer, the Ollama laptop, every ESP32/WLED node, every terminal tablet (the server-room terminal too, wave 7; the Server Door terminal, wave 11), ~~the chess phone~~ and the Quests; an **IP sheet** taped inside the server room.
- [ ] **HW-NET-03** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 B-3 — **One Mosquitto broker** on the **TV computer** (moved from the laptop; `brew install mosquitto`; `listener 1883 0.0.0.0`). Mosquitto 2.x refuses every remote client once a `listener` is defined unless you **also** set `allow_anonymous true` (isolated show LAN only) or a `password_file`. Allow mosquitto through the macOS firewall. Z2M, stagehand and every ESP32 point at it.
- [ ] **HW-NET-04** 🔴 P1 · @__ · 🎃 B-3 — Zigbee2MQTT running against that broker, with `availability` enabled (feeds the tech rail).
- [ ] **HW-NET-05** 🔴 P1 · @__ · 🎃 10-28 tech rehearsal — Load test: 8 screens (the server-room terminal included) + 2 Quests + the tent tablet + the Server Door terminal (wave 11; no chess phone) + any guests on the Wi-Fi, on the show AP **and through the uplink to the VPS** (SSE + 10 Hz poses ≈ modest), measured in the tent and the shed. **Oct 31:** the server-side load test moved before the freeze (SW-OPS-24, OCT31 K-32, 10-23); 10-28 is the on-site Wi-Fi recheck. Check mobile-data signal in every room (the basement, the server room, the tent) for guests (#16).
- [ ] **HW-NET-06** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 B-3 — **The TV computer and the Ollama laptop never sleep**: the TV computer hosts the broker, Z2M, the coordinator and the audio router, so a sleep drops every light, sensor and speaker; the laptop voices Trabolta. Run `caffeinate -dims` / `pmset` (or "prevent sleep on power adapter"), keep both on mains (UPS), and run clamshell only on external power. **Wave 20: Steph's laptop too** (the server-room terminal: Trabolta's face, the webcam scan and the M4 door sensor over USB): desktop Chrome on mains, `caffeinate -dims` under the kiosk launch, notifications off, lid closed only with the external display + mains; a sleep stops the door sensor (Run → Terminals: 🚪 fault `stale`), so 🚪 closed / opened carry the story until it wakes (HW-DOOR-11).
- [ ] **HW-NET-07** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 B-9 — **WAN failover set up and tested**: LTE as the router's second WAN with automatic failover, enough data for a night of SSE + TTS + media (precache media first, SW-SCR-25 / SW-AUD-02), and a measured switch-over time. Part of SW-OPS-19. — **Wave 9:** a director console now costs ≈ 0.12 Mbit/s (keyed patches), so a second console on the LTE failover no longer needs closing.
- [ ] **HW-NET-08** 🔴 P1 · @__ · 🎃 B-9 — **Router and uplink spares** (found by the Oct 31 show-safety review): failover covers the WAN, not the router; if the GL-MT3000 dies, the TV computer, the worker, the terminals and the ESP32 → broker link all go down. Keep the ISP router as a cold spare with the **same SSID/PSK**; set a **static IP on the TV computer itself** (it's the Mosquitto broker) and hard-code that address in the ESP32 firmware (N-08), so nothing depends on DHCP reservations; put the ISP modem/ONT on the UPS too; check the tethered phone's prepaid plan allows hotspot with enough data. Note: with the WAN down **no Act III automation fires** (door, patch bay, blackout, the house announcements all round-trip through the VPS), so any outage past the failover is Tier 0 (OCT31 W-31/W-32).

It sleeps on attract until The Server Room is open to everyone (The Heart or the alarm), then speaks Trabolta's lines in that room, ignores story slides and the countdown, and the TV mute doesn't silence it; a visit posts `terminal opened` / `terminal closed` (Trabolta's heart). The tent's reinstall tablet is a separate device (section F). | WLED controller, analog (Gledopto GL-C-211WL / Athom / QuinLED An-Penta) | 1 per run | €14–24 / ~$20 / see site | [QuinLED An-Penta](https://quinled.info/quinled-an-penta/) | 🔴 | @__ | Analog = one colour per strip: breathe/strobe/lightning OK, **no chase** |
| WLED controller, addressable (QuinLED Dig-Uno / Gledopto GL-C-015WL-D) | 1 per run | ~$20–30 | — | 🔴 | @__ | Needed for a moving Tube chase / "lines that lead you" |
| 24 V PSU: closed desktop brick with IEC inlet (Mean Well GST120A24 5 A per run; GST160A24 6.67 A) **or** LRS-100-24 per run / **LRS-200-24 (8.8 A) for two runs** | 1 per 1–2 runs | $20–35 | — | 🔴 | @__ | Size ×1.25 for **100% white** (WLED's ABL limiter does not apply to analog PWM outputs). Two 5 m runs at 14 W/m = 140 W × 1.25 = 175 W, so an LRS-150-24 (156 W) is **too small**. LRS units are open-frame with **exposed mains screw terminals**: prefer the closed bricks (barrel jacks are ~5 A max), or have a competent person wire the LRS with an earthed 3-core cord, strain relief and a terminal cover/enclosure; never a 2-core cord. If the strips are **12 V**: a 12 V PSU (a 12 V strip on 24 V dies instantly), 5 m × 14 W/m ≈ 5.8 A, 16 AWG, injection at both ends. The owned kits' own adapters can run one strip if rated ≥ 1.25 × its load. Keep off carpet and out of the tent |

QuinLED boards fuse internally |
| INMP441 I²S mic for WLED audio-reactive (optional) | 1 per audio-reactive node | ~$3–5 | — | 🔴 | @__ | Needs the WLED audio-reactive build; for `lights_dance` and the rave |

**Room plan (fill in counts after the walk-through).** Use these Z2M group names, which are the ones `stagehand/trapped.house.yaml` uses, plus `house` for everything and `blackout` for everything that may go dark. Avoid `/` in names. WLED device topics: `wled/tube`, `wled/living`, `wled/kitchen`, `wled/backyard`, `wled/registry`, and the group topic `wled/all` (no `wled/server`: the server room has no strip, w20b Q6). If a room mixes Hue and IKEA bulbs, add per-brand sub-groups (`living_hue` / `living_ikea`) for effects (HW-LIT-02). **Oct 31 wave 3:** the group ids stay as wired while the rooms took new names (`kitchen` = The Start Bar, `registry` = The RAM, `mudroom` = The Optical Drive). The five new rooms (The CPU, The Steam Room, The Fan Closet, The Graphics Card, The Hard Drive) have **no group yet**: their bulbs join `house`, so Trabolta can't cut their lights until a group, a `trapped.house.yaml` rule and an allow-list entry (`cast/trabolta.loom`) are added.

**Scenes per show cue.** This table is copied from `stagehand/trapped.house.yaml` (its header comment); the YAML is the source of truth, so change both together. One ID per cue (ID = the row number), WLED presets with the same ID on every node.

**Zigbee scenes are keyed by (group ID, scene ID).** Storing scene 3 on every room group does **not** make `scene_recall: 3` on the `house` group work. So: for a **whole-house** cue, set every room to the look, then `scene_store` that ID **on the `house` group itself**, and the show recalls it with one multicast. For a **room** cue (`living`, `backyard`, `server_room`), store it on that room's group. That keeps every cue at one Zigbee command (HW-LIT-07). Check per-bulb scene capacity: 15+ IDs across a room group plus `house` is a lot; test it in HW-LIT-02.

Name the groups per the room plan. Set **`power_on_behavior: previous`** (or `off`) on every bulb, or a tripped breaker or a bumped switch brings bulbs back at factory full white in the middle of a blackout. **Exception (Oct 31 critic pass): the safety bulbs** (stairs, hall, bathroom, the server-room path) get **`power_on_behavior: on`**, so a wall switch flipped during a blackout always brings them back (HW-SAFE-08).

The YAML uses only `breathe` and `blink` (both brands) for effects; if you want Hue-only effects, add per-brand sub-groups, or accept that IKEA bulbs stay static.

On Gledopto / Athom boards the ESP32 drives the MOSFETs directly, so use **2–5 kHz** (high PWM raises heat and, on ESP32 LEDC, lowers dimming bit depth; 2–5 kHz is still flicker-free on camera).

If it won't restore, re-pairing takes about an hour, so the night fallback is the wall switches (the safety bulbs come back ON, HW-SAFE-08).

## D. Server-room door ("alerts the app if opened when not unlocked")

**Wave 20 (2026-10-23) supersedes the sensor model below** (D1, D2, D16; the user: *"There is a door sensor (LDR + LED -- the door is "closed" when the LED is shining on the LDR, which reads about max value on an Arduino) … In the server room is Steph's laptop with an Arduino plugged into it (Grand Central M4) -- the door sensor is connected to it. This should transmit to the Loom server."*). The door sensor is an **LED on the frame shining into an LDR** (in a short black shroud tube) when the door is shut, read by an **Adafruit Grand Central M4** ([`hardware/server-room-m4/`](../../../hardware/server-room-m4); wave 22 renamed it from `server-room-door`): 12-bit reads, hysteresis + debounce, the build's `LED_MODE` (w20b, Q5, firmware `door-m4 1.1.0`): **`LED_STEADY`** (the default: the LED on **5V**, always lit, as wired today → `steady`, the raw reading) or **`LED_PULSED`** (the LED moved to **D2** → `mod`, lit − dark, room light can't fake *shut*), JSON lines at 115200 over USB to **Steph's laptop**, whose Chrome is the server-room terminal (`door=serial`; Web Serial; ⚙ *Connect door sensor* once). Each settled edge is `door_sensor_opened` / `_closed`; the story follows the 🚪 buttons instead once a director (or a room Antivirus) presses one, until 🚪 Trust the door sensor (D19). A blocking fault (`ldr_saturated`, `led_dead`, `no_port`, `stale`) posts nothing and shows red on Run → Terminals; `ldr_open` is a warning. **The ESP32 no longer reads the door** (D16): the copper-tape / wire-loop contact (HW-DOOR-06), the reed (HW-DOOR-01), the Zigbee contact (HW-DOOR-02) and their YAML rules (HW-DOOR-03) are retired; ~~the ESP32 keeps the patch bay, the ring, the siren and the relay~~ **w20b (Q7, *"No, just the Grand Central M4"*): no ESP32 in the server room at all** — the M4 is the room's only electronics, the patch bay is judged by the Antivirus watch (🔌 / 💥 / ❌, §E), the siren is the house router's, there is no ring; HW-DOOR-01 … 08 are history. The keypad lock (HW-DOOR-09) is unchanged and keeps the door locked (D17, Q9). The reed / SNZB-04P recommendation below is history.

NeoPixel: green shut, amber open, purple fault, blue until the laptop speaks. A reflash changes its USB PID in bootloader mode: grant the port once more |

- [ ] **HW-DOOR-10** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 wave 20 (D1, D2) — **The M4 door sensor**: wire the LED + LDR + shroud on the frame (the LED on the door side or the frame so the beam breaks when the door opens; never so it holds the latch), flash the sketch, run **`scripts/accept/N-20.sh`** (records `lit` / `dark` / `level` in `mod` and `steady` under room light and dark, the settle each needs, and proposes `close` / `open` / `settle`), then **`scripts/accept/K-05.sh`** (the LDR edges through the terminal into the story). Opening reads ≈ 150–450 ms after the beam breaks plus the cell's lag; shut after ≈ 1 s steady. Then the HW-DOOR-11 calibration on the laptop. **After any reflash** (the 1.1.0 build included) calibrate again: a reflash wipes the board's flash, and a wave 20 calibration stored as `auto` is not re-sent (⚙ reads *⚠ calibrate again*).
- [ ] **HW-DOOR-11** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 wave 20 (§5.3 step 6) — **The heart laptop** (Steph's laptop = the server-room terminal): the monitor as the main display + mouse + an external keyboard + the webcam under the screen; desktop Chrome on mains, `caffeinate`, notifications off (HW-NET-06). Launch Chrome **with the kiosk's own profile but without `--kiosk` once** (`--user-data-dir="$HOME/.loom-terminal" https://<site>/terminal/?code=<mod>&spot=server-room&door=serial&camera=user`), allow the camera, hold ⚙ 2 s → *Connect door sensor* → pick the Grand Central → *Calibrate: door shut*, open it, *Calibrate: door open* → Save; relaunch with the same `--user-data-dir` plus `--kiosk --autoplay-policy=no-user-gesture-required`. Pre-doors: a real open → shut → open watched on Run → Terminals (🚪 *open* / *shut* within a second, no override, no fault) and on the bulb, then 🔄 `reset_server_room` (and 🚪 Trust the door sensor if anyone pressed a 🚪). Runbook: [`docs/trapped/kiosk.md`](../../../docs/trapped/kiosk.md). On the HW-NET-02 IP sheet.

**Wave 7 (2026-10-17) supersedes the rest of this section's model** (decision #17 amended, below): the door has a real **standalone electronic keypad lock** (outside only; the code is set by hand on the lock to `facts.json` `door.code` and never networked; the inside handle always opens with no code, batteries or not; single-cylinder: egress never depends on power; the outside may stay locked on a dead battery, so an override key stays with the house manager). The sensor is **two loops of wire / copper tape that touch when the door opens** (closed = open) on GPIO19 of the server-room ESP32 (`DOOR_MODE DOOR_TAPE`, `TAPE_POLARITY kClosedWhenOpen`, open after ≈ 0.6 s, shut after ≈ 3 s; payload `{"open": bool, "tape": bool, "mode": "tape"}`); the reed is optional (`DOOR_REED` / `DOOR_BOTH`, `REED_POLARITY kClosedWhenShut`). **Wave 11:** the code comes from four digit notes (HW-PROP-13), a checkmate at the Server Door terminal or the majority (no riddle), and the first opening plays The Heart once Trabolta is awake, however the door was unlocked (🔧 first for a crew opening). **The code is a clean entry, whoever uses it: the door never alarms.** From the Y2K hour the first opening plays the server-room cutscene (The Heart); only the second wrong patch-bay combination alarms. A program gets the code from the riddle on the keypad sign, a checkmate from the Y2K hour, or the majority. Nothing in the story, the YAML or the ESP32 drives a lock; the ring shows `locked` (true) and the alarm only. The paragraphs below are the 2026-10-15 design, kept for the record.

**Decided 2026-10-15 (#17):** the **reed switch on the ESP32 is primary**, the **copper-tape contact (frame-bridge version) is the fallback** (HW-DOOR-06, now P1), and there is **no maglock** (a sensor and the indicator ring only; the door is never physically locked). The Zigbee contact sensor drops to an optional extra (HW-DOOR-02, P2). A disarm keypad isn't needed: the chess phone is the disarm path (#29, section J; wave 11: the Server Door terminal).

**Recommendation (as audited).** Primary: a **wired magnetic reed switch on the patch-bay ESP32** (one box owns the door, the indicator, the local siren and the patch bay, with one LWT `health/serverroom`). Fallback, run in parallel: a **Zigbee contact sensor** on the same door, preferably a **Sonoff SNZB-04P** (CR2477 cell, tamper detection) next to the mains router plug. An Aqara MCCGQ11LM also works, but Xiaomi/Aqara end devices are known to drop off some non-Xiaomi routers, so soak-test it for 24 h on the real mesh. **Not IKEA PARASOLL E2013**, which Z2M advises against. Both fire the same story signal `server_door_opened`, and the story's latch makes a double fire harmless.

**Your copper-tape idea, done right (the DIY option).** It works and looks diegetic if you change the geometry:
- Put **both** contacts on the frame: two parallel copper strips on the door-stop moulding, about 5 mm apart. On a hinged door the stop meets the door's **face** near the latch edge, so put **one bridging strip on the door face** where it seats against the stop. Closing the door shorts the two frame strips, and no wire runs to the moving door.
- Wire one frame strip to a GPIO, the other to GND. Closed = LOW, open = HIGH, so with no inversion the binary sensor is **ON = open**.
- Debounce: report "open" only once it has been stable for 300 ms, and "closed" after 50 ms. ESPHome: `filters: [delayed_on: 300ms, delayed_off: 50ms]` (on = open; swap them only if the pin is `inverted: true`).
- **The wire is long and guests touch the tape**: add an external 4.7–10 kΩ pull-up (the internal ~45 kΩ picks up noise on a long unshielded run), a 100 nF capacitor to GND at the ESP, and a **1 kΩ series resistor** (mandatory for copper tape, an ESD path from guests' hands; a small TVS diode is better still). The same input conditioning applies to the reed.
- Use tape with **conductive adhesive**, and press hard. The door is held only by its latch, so contact pressure is close to zero: that, more than the tape, is why foil-to-foil is unreliable, and a 1 mm gap reads "open". A phosphor-bronze weatherstrip, a door-jamb plunger switch (~$5) or a roller microswitch on the frame is the same circuit and far more repeatable. Any of them plugs into the reed's GPIO.

**How "unlocked" was decided before wave 7** (retired 2026-10-17: there is no "unlocked" any more; see the wave 7 note at the top of this section and OCT31.md §5 *Wave 7 status*):
- `Kernel.server_unlocked` starts false. It flips true at `majority_reached`: a faction has strictly more than half of all programs **and** its leader did their task (Excel planted the virus, or OpenOffice holds 20 facts), unless the director fires `waive_leader_tasks`. The director can also force `server_unlock`.
- The door opening while locked plays `Server Room Alarm` **once per 120 s** (latched `Night.alarm_on`; only the first alarm costs Trabolta composure): red lights, a siren, INTRUSION on the screens, Antivirus booth readouts, and an `!` alert to everyone.
- The door opening while unlocked: no alarm (`lights_server_open`). Non-members are policed by the Antivirus in person, because the sensor can't tell who opened the door.
- The door opening during a **chess disarm window** (a win on the chess phone from `Night.step >= 14` sets `Kernel.disarmed_until = Time.elapsed + 90`): no alarm, ring green; it re-locks when the window closes (SW-CHESS-01).
- Wrong patch combos trip the alarm on the second wrong combo, or on any wrong combo while the room is locked.

## E. Trabolta's heart: the video patch bay

**Wave 22 (2026-10-24): the bay judges itself.** 15 jacks (8 sources V1–V8 on top, 7 inputs D1–D7 below, no decoys) are read by the Grand Central M4 beside the door sensor, and the server-room terminal on Steph's laptop posts `patch_changed {combo}` for each new complete patch; the story judges it against the Kernel. The watch presses 🔌 💥 ❌ only when the bay missed one, and a press puts the bay on the buttons (`Night.patch_override`, *Patch bay on buttons* on the glance) until 🔌 *Trust the patch bay again* (`trust_patch_bay`). Combo A moved to V6 → D7 (was D8). The paragraph below is wave 20b's, kept for the record. **Wave 20b (2026-10-23, Q7: *"No, just the Grand Central M4"*): no ESP32 is installed for Oct 31.** The bay was a prop nothing scans: the Antivirus on the patch-bay watch judges the cables and presses 🔌 *Combo A is in* / 💥 *Combo B is in* / ❌ *wrong combination* (the `radio_*` cues; `scripts/accept/K-05.sh` presses 🔌 unless `ESP32=1`); the siren is the house router's `sound alarm_siren`. The rows and items below are the ESP32 design, kept for the record (`hardware/server-room/`, its YAML rules kept, unused).

**What the story needs.** The rewrite's combos use 8 "fixed voltage" jacks on top (`V1…V8`) and 8 "DAC" jacks below (`D1…D8`). **Combo A** = `V2-D5,V4-D1,V6-D7,V7-D3` (wave 22; was `…V6-D8…` on the 8 + 8 design) disables Trabolta ("take the grease out of the lightning"). **Combo B** = `V1-D6,V3-D2,V5-D7,V8-D4` destroys the computer. Each combo is split into **codex pieces** that the majority faction earns. The `Kernel.combo_*` seeds, the codex halves and the YAML strings must match, and SW-SH-02 tests that.

**Before anything: bench-test the bay** (multimeter on the rear BNCs; on an intact bay the rear connectors are the only electrical access, and a front plug connects the cord to its rear):
- centre-to-shell ≈ 75 Ω means the bay is **self-terminated**. An empty jack then reads "connected" against any pull-up, and it ruins a voltage ladder too. **Test twice**: once with nothing plugged in, and again with a cord in one front jack while you measure its partner's rear centre-to-shell. On many self-terminating bays the termination switches in **only when a plug breaks the normal**, so the empty test reads open and misses it; a terminated jack then reads LOW on every scan and looks patched to everything.
- top-N centre to bottom-N centre ≈ 0 Ω means **normalled**. Every column then reads as already patched.
- Fix, in order of preference: swap to non-normalled, non-terminated jack modules; open the bay and cut the normal links and termination resistors; or treat the front panel as a façade and solder your own wire to each jack's centre, with all shells on GND.

**Decided 2026-10-15 (#18): digital all-pairs sensing.** Cable order and Combo B's trigger (cables or a MAIN POWER switch) weren't answered, so the combos stay **unordered cable sets** and Combo B stays on cables (HW-PATCH-08 optional). The voltage fiction (labels, meters) stays as dressing.

**Why I'd sense it digitally, not with voltages (kindly).** Your voltage idea is thematically perfect, and you can keep it as the fiction: labels, meters and real DACs driving needles. As a *sensor*, though, it has problems:
- To identify a patch, the bottom row has to be **read** by an ADC. Two DAC outputs patched together just fight each other.
- With Wi-Fi on only ADC1 works (ADC2 is blocked), and on a WROOM-32 DevKitC only **6** ADC1 pins are broken out (GPIO32, 33, 34, 35, 36/VP, 39/VN; 37/38 aren't). They are nonlinear at both ends too.
- 75 Ω terminations and old, oxidised jacks shift the levels.
- A ladder can't see top-to-top patches at all.

The digital scan is cheaper and unambiguous:
- Drive one jack LOW at a time, with every other jack on a pull-up input. Any jack that reads LOW is connected to the driven jack by a cable.
- Each jack takes one plug, so there's no ghosting and no diodes are needed.
- It sees **every** patch, including the "wrong" ones that should trip the alarm.

**The voltage version (not chosen, kept for reference):**
- 8 top jacks fed from a resistor ladder off 3.3 V at distinct levels about 300 mV apart (0.3, 0.6 … 2.4 V).
- Each of the 8 bottom jacks gets a **~1 MΩ pulldown on the jack side** (an empty jack reads 0 V) and a small **1–10 kΩ series resistor** to an **ADS1115** channel (2 boards × 4 channels, I²C). Don't use 100 kΩ series + 100 kΩ pulldown: that is a 2:1 divider, the levels land 150 mV apart, and a ±100 mV window overlaps its neighbours. Buffered taps also work.
- Firmware maps each reading to the nearest level within **±75 mV or less**, using a calibration table made with your actual cables.
- It still needs the bay de-terminated and de-normalled, and it can't detect top-to-top patches.

The plug's shell-to-GND short is unreliable (an unpowered stick's shell floats). Hardware can't know *who* planted, so it's flavour (`virus_inserted`) or the director confirms; the guest button `plant_the_virus` stays the source of truth (P2) |

(2) **Retire alexa**: `install-alexa.sh --uninstall`, retire the old `house.yaml` (`trapped.house.local.yaml` overlay), check the `show` job's `--config`. (3) `uv run stagehand sounds placeholders` → `uv run stagehand check --config trapped.house.yaml --only audio` (32 / 30, the layout not moved, the placeholders listed) → `uv run stagehand launchd install --job audio`. (4) A 🔈 Test on each speaker (map editor → 🔊 Audio); an announcement through the router (`stagehand announce "…"`, *sent to the router*, never *no router is listening*); a story sound from a placeholder; ■ Stop from Run, a host phone and MQTT with the uplink down (SW-AUD-14). `scripts/accept/K-20.sh` walks it.

Define the Z2M `blackout` group and WLED segments to leave them out, and point `lights_blackout` at it (today it hits `house` = everything, SW-SH-01). Glow tape on steps, staff flashlights, a clear path out of the pitch-black Optical Drive (the mud room) and the dark Tube. **Oct 31 critic pass:** the server room and the route from it to the living room are spared too (guests are *in* the server room when Combo A/B blacks out the house); see K-05, HW-SAFE-07, HW-SAFE-08.
- [ ] **HW-SAFE-02** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 safety walk 10-30 — **Strobes**: every flashing preset (`lights_alarm`, `lights_rave`, the glitch/lightning presets, `screens_flicker`) stays **≤ 3 Hz** and avoids full-field saturated red flashing (WCAG / Ofcom photosensitive guidance). **Oct 31 critic pass:** WLED ps 7 (glitch "Lightning / strobe"), ps 12 (alarm red strobe), ps 17 (rave strobe / rainbow) and ps 21 (Lightning playlist; since wave 9 nothing sends it) all exceed it: rebuild them with Breathe / Fade / Running, or Blink ≤ 3 Hz, stopwatch-tested 10-22; run the free **PEAT** tool (Trace Center) on the ad MP4, the attract loop, the screensaver recording and any lyric video before 10-27. Put a flashing-lights warning on the invite and the door; never strobe in the tent (HW-TENT-03).
- [ ] **HW-SAFE-03** 🔴 P0 · @__ · 🎃 safety walk 10-30 — **Mains near water and outdoors**: GFCI/RCD-protected outlets (or inline GFCI) for the tent, the Truth Pong flood, the backyard and anything near the bobbing tub; outdoor-rated cords with connections off the ground; an IP65+ flood. **Oct 31 critic pass (tent power):** **12 AWG** outdoor cords (14 AWG minimum; 16 AWG overheats at 1500 W), fully uncoiled, drip loops, connections in a dry box; the radiator on low, ≥ 1 m from the fabric, on a different house circuit from the tent electronics; a breaker map by 10-24 (which circuit carries the kettle and the speakers); GFCIs tested with a receptacle/GFCI tester on 10-24 and 10-30. "Out of splash range" alone isn't enough. LED PSUs closed or enclosed and earthed (HW-LIT-05).
- [ ] **HW-SAFE-04** 🔴 P1 · @__ · 🎃 safety walk 10-30 — **Weather plan** for the outdoor tent and Truth Pong (decided #23/#31: the tent is really outside; **the shed is the bad-weather fallback**, HW-TENT-06 + SW-MAP-13): rain cover for the table, a heater for the tent or shed, cold Quest batteries (keep spares warm inside) and heat (no direct sun on headsets). Make the tent/shed call from the forecast the morning of (SW-OPS-10). **Move to the shed on gusts > 30–40 km/h**, not only on a 40 km/h sustained wind (pop-up canopies lift earlier).
- [ ] **HW-SAFE-05** 🔴 P1 · @__ · 🎃 safety walk 10-30 — **Safety walk** on install day: first aid and the extinguisher placed, egress clear of cables (tent EXIT sign), the server-room door opens freely from inside (wave 7: the keypad lock is on the outside only; the inside handle opens with no code, batteries in or out; the override key and spare batteries with the house manager; no lock rules in the YAML, and its test forbids them), every cable run taped, including the speaker runs (HW-AUD-09). **Oct 31 critic pass:** real gear in the server room (router, UPS, dongle, ESP32 power) out of reach and taped off from the patch bay; the **emergency card** (civic address, 911, the nearest ER — QEII Halifax Infirmary, check the address — extinguisher and first-aid locations, `ALL STOP`) posted at the door and on every crew card; LED candles only; FR fabric or spray on the Tube; a towel + mat under the bobbing tub; the neighbours told, the backyard quiet late.
- [ ] **HW-SAFE-06** 🔴 P1 · @__ · 🎃 OP-01 — **Halloween porch** (the party is Oct 31): HRM trick-or-treating runs about 18:00–20:00 and the Optical Drive (the mud room) is the front door. The invitation says arrive after 8 pm; a candy bowl and a sign on the porch; the Optical Drive dim, not dark, until 20:00, so no child walks into a pitch-black room.
- [ ] **HW-SAFE-07** 🟡 P0 · @__ · 🎃 K-05 — **Trabolta's room blackouts are safe** (found by the Oct 31 show-safety review, verified): his AI-triggered `cut_the_lights` allow-list includes **The Cloud (backyard)** and **The Cache (kitchen; The Start Bar since wave 3)** (`cast/trabolta.loom` ≈ 171), which darkens the outdoor steps and the tent path, with Antivirus walking captured guests through it, plus a kitchen with knives and a stove, for 90 s. Drop The Cloud from the list (+ a story test); the kitchen goes to about 20%, not OFF (the YAML payload); the tent-path flood on a plug that isn't in any group. Delete the YAML's maglock rules (≈ lines 350-357) so a stray `server_room_lock` device can never lock a door with people inside. — **Oct 31 wave 1:** on master (not yet deployed) (K-05: safety rooms out of `house`, WLED off per device, Tube on dim preset 22, 0d1e388), device check: put the safety bulbs outside `house` with `power_on_behavior: on`, build WLED preset 22, then the `scripts/accept/K-05.sh` blackout step.

People open a heavy door into a small room full of cables: check at the 10-28 tech that the room's darkest look still shows the floor and the door handle, and the battery motion-sensor lights below cover the way out (one just inside the server-room door is cheap insurance).) **Safety light independent of every system** (found by the Oct 31 show-safety review): cheap **battery motion-sensor LED lights** on every stair, the server-room → living-room path, the back steps and the tent path (OCT31 A-17); `power_on_behavior: on` on the stairs, hall, bathroom and path bulbs (with `previous`, cycling a wall switch during a blackout returns them to OFF); and an **`ALL STOP`** radio call word: admin cue 💡 Restore the house lights (`restore_the_lights`) + QLab panic (Esc) + TV voice mute, then the wall switches, with the house manager as safety lead (OCT31 OP-20). Drilled at the 10-28 tech.
- [ ] **HW-SAFE-09** 🔴 P1 · @__ · 🎃 B-6 — **VR safety** (found by the Oct 31 show-safety review): thumbstick movement, a dark tent and drinking guests add up to falls and motion sickness. Play **seated** (a chair), snap turn, a spotter (a tent assistant, since Julia can't spot while running the tent tablet and voicing the Installation Wizard), headsets wiped between players.

29. ✅ **RESOLVED: the disarm path is a chess phone.** A phone taped to the wall outside the server room plays chess against a very strong engine. A win from `Night.step >= 14` opens a 90 s window in which the door opens without the alarm. → new section 12 (SW-CHESS-01..07), section J (HW-CHESS-01); SW-STORY-14 merged into SW-CHESS-01. The app is MIT; Stockfish lives in its own GPL-3.0-or-later package (`chess/engine/`). **Wave 4 (U15–U17):** the disarm is in the simulation — the King on the tent tablet (SW-KING-01..05) fires the same `chess won`; the phone stays the fallback until the King is device-checked. **Amended 2026-10-17 (wave 7):** there is no disarm and no 90 s window. A checkmate from `Night.step >= 14` (the King, or ♟ for a win seen on the phone) gives the winner **the door code** (*The Door Code* in their Codex, once); the riddle on the keypad sign and the majority are the other two ways to it; only the second wrong patch-bay combination alarms. SW-STORY-14 is answered. **Amended again 2026-10-18 (wave 11):** the chess game is only on **the Server Door terminal** outside the door, all night, no hints (a win gives the code until 📄); the King's strength is his shared health in the reinstallation games; the riddle is gone and the four digit notes are the other path; the chess phone (HW-CHESS-01, U17) is retired.

Was: **OPEN (asked 2026-10-15, needed by 10-21 for OCT31 K-04): may guest DMs to Trabolta go to a hosted model as a fallback?** If the Ollama laptop or its worker dies, `agents.fallback.yaml` can point the same persona at a hosted OpenAI-compatible endpoint, run from any laptop: zero-code insurance, but guests' DM text goes to a third party. **Default if unanswered: no**; build SW-AI-24 / N-11 (the hub posts the hold line) and the director answers key DMs.
